Decision: `D-2026-10-13-s4-bounded-financial-basis`. The owner selected `A-S4-financial` in the 2026-10-13 next-work contest; the Codex Architect resolves its S4-02 contract in `.agent/architect-A-S4-financial-answers.md`. A Site-scoped `FinancialAssumptionRevision` has immutable scope/key/revision, ISO currency and canonical unit, Decimal value, explicit authored/contract/recorded/scenario basis, half-open valid time and recorded/superseded time. Writes derive human `author_id` and actor/authorization metadata from a verified server principal under `A-actor-identity`; absent authentication disables human editing, while an explicitly labelled server-authorized SYSTEM fixture may publish with service/job provenance. No default price, silent tenant fallback, overlapping current validity, client-asserted author or unlabelled retroactive edit is permitted. The first key is Site diesel price per litre; maintenance needs its own rate/source and matching quantity and cannot be inferred as actual expenditure from candidate runtime.

S4-02 may derive a modelled, assumption-conditioned **potential fuel-cost exposure** only for an exact supported mini-grid FindingRevision and its selected assessment/evidence/configuration basis. `dispatch-3.1` publishes no fuel-saving quantity: a separately reviewed versioned fuel derivation must use accepted generator-power coverage for candidate segments and the selected Foundation's L/kWh consumption property, with explicit uncertainty/bounds and no private Lab truth, unsupported interpolation or pricing of Indeterminate/Necessary time. Candidate time proves no saved liters. The lower bound on potentially avoidable fuel is zero; an upper amount is shown only under a defensible stated model bound. A price valid for the entire consequence window and known at the selection cutoff multiplies unrounded compatible Decimal quantity bounds; displayed lows round down and highs up to currency minor units. No price yields `UNPRICED` with null amount, never zero; unsupported quantity, mismatched unit/currency/period/basis, stale projection and historical supersession receive distinct honest states.

Each append-only `FinancialConsequenceRevision` stores exact FindingRevision, primary and supporting assessment revisions, technical quantity bounds and source/basis digest, every assumption revision used, formula version, overlap group, half-open window, amount bounds/currency and limitations. An explicit backdated price correction valid over the old window appends a new assumption and consequence for the same FindingRevision and quantity; a future price does not reprice yesterday, and neither edit mutates the Finding or pinned old consequence. S4-02 persists and reads Site-scoped assumption/consequence current and exact history with actor, validity, lineage, pagination and typed refusal/pending states. After review it may support `financials:1#a/2#a` and `assumptions:1#a/3#a`; `mini:4#a` waits for S5 composition. Decision: `D-2026-10-14-action-lifecycle-and-policy-application`. Under the owner's 08:40-10:40 +0300 delegation, the Codex Architect chooses and resolves `A-action-lifecycle` in the 0.54% next-work contest (`.agent/next-work-2026-10-14d.json`: evidence-acceptance consequence work 2.020, Action lifecycle 2.009). The scores are effectively tied. This bounded architecture decision can close now and removes the common S6 Action gate across roughly fifteen W1 core portions; the evidence-acceptance consequence implementation still needs a Planner packet and remains a hard prerequisite of S5-01's money read. The next verified report, rather than this contest snapshot, routes the next work. No S6 UI owner checkpoint is waived.

S6-01 creates one immutable, tenant/Site-scoped Action identity from a reviewed persisted `FindingRevision` under the canonical Finding route. It pins the originating revision, selected assessment and accepted-evidence/configuration basis; a later Finding revision does not silently rewrite that origin. A Finding may have several Actions, but each accepted recommendation creates or idempotently returns one Action for its server-defined command key. The recommendation is a bounded proposed reserve-SOC policy trial with explicit purpose, limitation and source Finding; it is not a measured saving or verified outcome. The Action detail root is `/sites/{site}/actions/{action_id}` with stable child routes and exact `event_id` selection, and Site/Finding back-links. S6-01 may navigate from a known Finding or Action ID before the separate S6-03 Site ActionIndex exists. Hidden unsupported tabs must not masquerade as empty delivered views.

Action state is derived from an append-only, server-ordered `ActionEvent` ledger. Record stable event ID, Action and Site, event type, `occurred_at`, server `recorded_at`, idempotency key, actor kind/ID, source channel, authorization basis and triggering command/job or content revision. Idempotent identical retries return the event; conflicting reuse and cross-Site references fail. Historical reads select a recorded cutoff and then `(recorded_at,id)` order; a backdated `occurred_at` is displayed as such but never rewrites the audit order. `PROPOSED`/`ACCEPTED`, application `PENDING`/`APPLIED`/`FAILED`, and later verification status are separate dimensions. Acceptance does not assert application; an applied change or completed work does not assert benefit. A failed application preserves its attempt, error and retry relation without an effective-policy claim. No human author, approval or attestation is inferred from a gateway credential, client field, local OS user or SYSTEM fixture. Under the existing `A-actor-identity` rule, human writes need a verified operator principal; otherwise only a clearly labelled, server-authorized SYSTEM demonstration command with stable service/job provenance is allowed, or the write stays unavailable.

The supported S6-01 change is a controlled reserve-SOC edit through S1 Foundation publish, producing a new immutable effective `FoundationRevision` and an immutable change artifact that links Action, accepted recommendation, authorized command, old/new revision IDs, exact field/value/unit, validity and recorded time, and the application event. Validate the expected old revision and Site scope under the publish lock; reject stale, unauthorized or invalid edits and record a failed attempt without claiming the new setting took effect. `APPLIED` requires confirmation of the persisted new effective revision; an ActionEvent alone is insufficient. The old basis and every event/change revision remain addressable after reload. Do not mutate a Lab-only override or rewrite accepted evidence. S6-01's Execution/History and applied `changes:1#a` show policy activity with event and configuration provenance, including pending/failed cases; they do not show an effect verdict.

S6-02 owns the separate immutable `VerificationPlanRevision` and later `VerificationOutcome`. A plan pins this Action, the applied-change event and new Foundation revision, a pre-change baseline assessment/evidence basis, target, service/reserve guardrails, required coverage and later-window selection rule before evaluation. Use separate baseline and later Lab runs under their respective fixed effective Foundation revisions; commit their public reports through the ordinary gateway and central acceptance. Evaluate a closed later accepted window against the frozen plan and baseline, with exact analytic/evidence IDs. Missing or conflicting evidence yields `INCONCLUSIVE`; failed guardrail prevents `VERIFIED` even if the target improves; later results append/supersede without rewriting prior outcomes. Plan/evidence/verification child routes preserve exact plan and outcome IDs. `A-S6-verification` remains open for the full comparison rule; this lifecycle decision does not resolve it. S6-03 alone owns the Site-scoped ActionIndex/list and its watermarked empty/positive states; `A-action-index` remains open. Planner must refine S6-01's event transition table, command/API schema, actor path, race/idempotency checks, Foundation publish seam, stored fixtures and desktop/narrow Action-root states against the v6.9 Action screens and technical design before implementation.

Order an attested channel by each reading's `placed_at` instant (measurement interval end, otherwise source time) +/- the attested error, not by the full measurement interval widened by error. Measurement intervals that only touch do not overlap; genuine measurement-interval overlap or overlapping placement-uncertainty intervals in one channel remains `REPORTS_OVERLAP`/ambiguous. Where placement-uncertainty intervals are disjoint, gateway admission sequence disagreement is a late arrival, not `SEQUENCE_ORDER_INVERTED`; sequence still resolves identity and duplicates. Retain epoch ambiguity unless the existing explicit cohort selection is used; automatic cross-epoch continuity needs separate review. Attestation changes enqueue all affected UTC-day windows through the matching six-hour query bracket and neighboring continuity windows, preserving immutable runs and selections. Proven Candidate spans may remain `SUPPORTED`, while a material refused tail produces a new FindingRevision with `GENERATOR_SERIES_AMBIGUOUS` and `SERIES_LIMIT`. A shared read-derived money eligibility gate withholds current amount during pending reassessment or material unresolved generator-series ambiguity, even if a historical consequence is `CURRENT PRICED`; eligibility alone does not replace the exact-head consequence and source/currentness checks. The Planner must add the corrected overlap rule, requeue scope, eligibility precedence and explicit-epoch boundary to the LR-01 packet before implementation. Authority: `tasks/LR-01-late-reading-series-and-money-limit.md`, `.agent/S4-02c-review.md` F1, and `D-2026-10-14-s402c-late-sequence-and-s5-money`.

For LR-01, any same-asset generator-role series limit intersecting the episode +/- 6 h bracket withholds current money, even without Candidate-tail overlap; exact limit lineage must remain visible. The shared Finding/Financials money eligibility gate stays unchanged by schedule health. Rule 8 must admit only this ninth writer's INSERTs to its tables, reject Action-table writes by every other module and reject mutation or DDL in the Action adapter while retaining all eight existing protections. Authorized same-Site failures remain separate durable attempts after rollback. Include the pinned FoundationRevision in recommendation identity and persist the immutable accepted-recommendation snapshot; after acceptance, recommendation read returns it with the Action link and a separate live status. A server-authorized, state-neutral SYSTEM demonstration `OBSERVED` event with fixed `DEMO_REVIEW_NOTE` code may carry an explicit `occurred_at` between ACCEPTED and server `recorded_at`, no more than 30 days earlier, to prove backdated history without changing application state. SYSTEM demonstration commands require a server-issued scoped capability and explicit label. The full M1-M3 ruling is in `.agent/architect-S6-01-answers.md` under "Follow-up (plan review M1-M3)". S6-01a/S6-01b packets must incorporate the named changes before implementation; S6-01b still waits for the owner's actual visual walkthrough before merge.

# Architect rulings - S4-05 stored demonstration provenance (2026-10-15)

Authority: `tasks/S4-05-stored-demo-provenance.md`, `I-stored-demo-provenance`, `.agent/architect-S4-02-batch-answers.md` Q3, `D-2026-10-15-lr01-source-time-cohort`, `D-2026-10-15-s501-finding-choice`, and the owner's absence delegation. These are implementation decisions, not owner approval of visible copy or pilot designation.

## Q1 - authoritative publication moment: accept with transaction detail

The only authoritative authored-demonstration designation is a server-authorized SYSTEM publication committed **before** admission of its exact Batch V2. Pin tenant/Site UUID, gateway/source, external batch ID, canonical payload SHA-256, immutable publication ID and fixture/spec ID/version. Resolve service principal, authorization basis, channel, trigger and recorded time on the server. An identical registration retry returns the same ID; changed content or reuse of a publication ID fails. A gateway credential, client text, `SIMULATED`, Site/day match or missing designation proves no authorship.

Registration and `open_batch` must take the same transaction-scoped lock on the scoped batch key and check for an existing batch while holding it. Registration after any batch admission fails, including a partially completed batch. `open_batch` binds at most the exact preauthorized commitment in its admission transaction; a matching-key but mismatched commitment fails rather than silently becoming unknown. Accepted IDs inherit that immutable binding, including after chunk restart. Duplicates retain their original accepted ID and provenance; rejected/conflict rows acquire none. Existing accepted rows remain unknown, with no retrospective spec/date backfill. The fixture command stays server-side with an explicit configured authority; no public designation route.

**Packet change:** spell out the shared lock key/transaction, registration-after-partial-admission refusal, and mismatch failure in the phase-1 contract/tests. Show the P01 positive path as fresh publication followed by upload of that exact new batch.

## Q2 - several publications in one Finding: accept with set definition

Several distinct authored publication IDs may support one selected FindingRevision if **every** cited accepted ID resolves within the requested tenant/Site to an `AUTHORED_DEMONSTRATION` publication with the same fixture/spec ID **and version**. Return all distinct publication IDs and per-ID provenance. Different fixture IDs or versions, or a combination of designated and undesignated IDs, is `MIXED`. An empty citation set, no designated IDs, or any missing, foreign-Site or broken accepted-ID/publication link is `UNKNOWN`; a broken link takes precedence over `MIXED`, and the read names the problematic IDs. An accepted ID with no designation is unknown authorship, not proof of a real source.

Compute a Finding from its selected immutable FindingRevision evidence set. Compute each consequence revision from the union of that selected FindingRevision set and that consequence revision's own quantified power-interval accepted IDs. Report the two sets and their IDs separately so a later consequence can have a different state; never inherit the current head's label for historical revisions.

**Packet change:** add a precise set-state truth table, including two publications with one fixture/version, differing versions, empty set, missing/foreign link and consequence-only mixed input. Require response fields for all publication IDs, per-evidence provenance, problematic IDs and exact selected revision IDs.

## Q3 - unknown source and money: accept with gate precedence

`MIXED` or `UNKNOWN` provenance blocks a **current displayed amount** on Finding Financials, consequence and S5 Overview, even if a stored consequence says `CURRENT PRICED` or LR-01 money eligibility says `ELIGIBLE`. Keep any old value reachable only as explicitly historical/audit material with its exact revision, source state and limitation; it is not a current amount. Source provenance is one independent gate: an all-authored set may show current money only when LR-01 eligibility, exact selected Finding/consequence basis, currentness, source-open and ordinary price/quantity gates also pass. If several gates fail, expose their typed reasons without hiding the source problem. Do not treat unknown as real measurements or as zero cost. Keep `Demonstration data` only for the fully authored case. Mixed/unknown copy remains proposed for the owner at the W1 checkpoint; this ruling does not approve wording or pilot designation.

**Packet change:** state the gate composition and audit-only historical behavior for all three money reads, add tests for `CURRENT PRICED` plus `UNKNOWN`/`MIXED`, LR `ELIGIBLE` plus source refusal, and S5 composition after its merge. Include the owner-visible copy and pilot designation in the checkpoint handoff.

## PostgreSQL writer and rule 8

This extends `evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py`, the existing ledger writer; it creates no new writer. Add the publication table and any separate binding table to the ledger's rule-8 ownership allowlist **for INSERT only**. If binding is instead a nullable `ingestion_batch.publication_id`, set it only in the batch INSERT during admission; never permit an UPDATE to add or change it. Rule 8 must reject UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE/upsert/DDL on publication or link rows from the ledger and reject their writes from every other module. Preserve existing writer allowlists, reverse ownership and driver/write non-vacuity, including any independently merged writer when implementation starts. Back this up with database uniqueness, scoped foreign keys and immutability enforcement so direct SQL cannot change publication/link rows; static guard alone is insufficient. Migration owns DDL, not a runtime writer. If LR-01 or another merged slice takes migration 0014 first, re-chain and renumber on the actual single head.

**Packet change:** name the INSERT-only rule-8 and database immutability proof for whichever binding design is chosen. Add permitted-ledger-INSERT, forbidden-other-writer-INSERT, ledger/other UPDATE/DELETE, upsert, omitted-writer/import and client-route designation probes to phase 1. Do not weaken existing eight-writer protections; account for the Action writer if merged before this implementation.

 succeeded in 961ms:
/**
 * The T028 product vocabulary guard (D-2026-10-04-plain-language-analysis-views,
 * `.agent/T028-architect-presentation.md` section 4).
 *
 * It scans what a screen actually says - every rendered text node, including
 * text a backend delivered, graphics' captions, legends and `<title>`s - and
 * every accessible label an assistive technology would read (`aria-label`,
 * `title`, `alt`, `placeholder`, `aria-description`), against the versioned
 * list in `productVocabulary.json`. Matching is on word boundaries, so `run`
 * does not match `truncate`, and a failure names the page and the phrase.
 *
 * Exclusions are only the three the decision names, applied to one element at
 * a time with `data-vocabulary-exempt="<reason>"`: an exact source-owned
 * reference value, a user-owned name, or a Lab entry. An exempt element must
 * wrap a single value - one with element children is itself a violation - so a
 * whole panel or a backend response cannot be exempted by marking its root.
 *
 * The guard is a regression aid. A person still has to judge plain meaning,
 * uncertainty and claim strength.
 */
import vocabulary from "./productVocabulary.json";

export interface VocabularyViolation {
  page: string;
  phrase: string;
  /** Where the phrase was found: `text` or the attribute name. */
  where: string;
  text: string;
}

const LABEL_ATTRIBUTES = [
  "aria-label",
  "aria-description",
  "aria-roledescription",
  "title",
  "alt",
  "placeholder",
] as const;

export const EXEMPT_ATTRIBUTE = "data-vocabulary-exempt";
/**
 * Exact source-owned reference values inside authored text, on the element
 * that renders them, separated by " | ". The scan removes exactly those values
 * from that element's text and labels and still scans the authored words
 * around them. A value must have the shape of an identifier (letters, digits,
 * `.`, `_`, `-`, joined by " / ", optionally " vN"), so prose cannot be
 * passed off as a reference.
 */
export const REFERENCE_ATTRIBUTE = "data-vocabulary-reference";
const REFERENCE_SHAPE = /^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+(?: \/ [A-Za-z0-9._-]+)*(?: v\d+)?$/;
export const VOCABULARY_VERSION: number = vocabulary.version;
export const EXEMPTION_REASONS = Object.keys(vocabulary.exemptions);

interface Term {
  phrase: string;
  pattern: RegExp;
}

const TERMS: Term[] = vocabulary.terms.map((term) => ({
  phrase: term.phrase,
  pattern: new RegExp(
    `(?<![\\p{L}\\p{N}])${escape(term.phrase).replace(/ /g, "\\s+")}(?![\\p{L}\\p{N}])`,
    "caseSensitive" in term && term.caseSensitive ? "u" : "iu",
  ),
}));

const GLOSSES = vocabulary.glosses.map(
  (gloss) => new RegExp(escape(gloss.text), "giu"),
);

function escape(text: string): string {
  return text.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, "\\$&");
}

/** The forbidden phrases one piece of text contains, after allowed glosses. */
export function forbiddenPhrases(text: string): string[] {
  let scanned = text;
  for (const gloss of GLOSSES) {
    scanned = scanned.replace(gloss, " ");
  }
  return TERMS.filter((term) => term.pattern.test(scanned)).map((term) => term.phrase);
}

function references(element: Element | null): string[] {
  const holder = element?.closest(`[${REFERENCE_ATTRIBUTE}]`) ?? null;
  const raw = holder?.getAttribute(REFERENCE_ATTRIBUTE) ?? "";
  return raw.split(" | ").filter((value) => value.length > 0 && REFERENCE_SHAPE.test(value));
}

function withoutReferences(text: string, values: string[]): string {
  let out = text;
  for (const value of values) out = out.split(value).join(" ");
  return out;
}

function exemptReason(element: Element | null): string | null {
  const exempt = element?.closest(`[${EXEMPT_ATTRIBUTE}]`) ?? null;
  return exempt === null ? null : exempt.getAttribute(EXEMPT_ATTRIBUTE);
}

/**
 * Every vocabulary violation inside `root`, labelled with `page`.
 *
 * Also returns how many text pieces were scanned, so a caller can prove the
 * scan saw a populated screen rather than an empty or loading one.
 */
export function scanProductVocabulary(
  root: HTMLElement,
  page: string,
): { violations: VocabularyViolation[]; scanned: number } {
  const violations: VocabularyViolation[] = [];
  let scanned = 0;

  for (const element of Array.from(root.querySelectorAll(`[${EXEMPT_ATTRIBUTE}]`))) {
    const reason = element.getAttribute(EXEMPT_ATTRIBUTE) ?? "";
    if (!EXEMPTION_REASONS.includes(reason)) {
      violations.push({ page, phrase: `unknown exemption "${reason}"`, where: EXEMPT_ATTRIBUTE, text: element.outerHTML.slice(0, 160) });
    } else if (element.children.length > 0) {
      violations.push({ page, phrase: "exemption wraps more than one value", where: EXEMPT_ATTRIBUTE, text: element.outerHTML.slice(0, 160) });
    }
  }

  for (const element of Array.from(root.querySelectorAll(`[${REFERENCE_ATTRIBUTE}]`))) {
    const raw = element.getAttribute(REFERENCE_ATTRIBUTE) ?? "";
    for (const value of raw.split(" | ")) {
      if (!REFERENCE_SHAPE.test(value)) {
        violations.push({ page, phrase: "a reference value is not an identifier", where: REFERENCE_ATTRIBUTE, text: value });
      }
    }
  }

  const walker = root.ownerDocument.createTreeWalker(root, NodeFilter.SHOW_TEXT);
  for (let node = walker.nextNode(); node !== null; node = walker.nextNode()) {
    const text = (node.textContent ?? "").trim();
    if (text.length === 0) continue;
    const parent = node.parentElement;
    if (parent !== null && ["SCRIPT", "STYLE"].includes(parent.tagName)) continue;
    if (exemptReason(parent) !== null) continue;
    scanned += 1;
    for (const phrase of forbiddenPhrases(withoutReferences(text, references(parent)))) {
      violations.push({ page, phrase, where: "text", text });
    }
  }

  const elements = [root, ...Array.from(root.querySelectorAll("*"))];
  for (const element of elements) {
    if (exemptReason(element) !== null) continue;
    for (const attribute of LABEL_ATTRIBUTES) {
      const value = element.getAttribute(attribute);
      if (value === null || value.trim().length === 0) continue;
      scanned += 1;
      for (const phrase of forbiddenPhrases(withoutReferences(value, references(element)))) {
        violations.push({ page, phrase, where: attribute, text: value });
      }
    }
  }

  return { violations, scanned };
}

/** A failure message naming each page, phrase and the text it was found in. */
export function describeViolations(violations: VocabularyViolation[]): string {
  return violations
    .map((v) => `${v.page}: "${v.phrase}" in ${v.where}: ${v.text.slice(0, 200)}`)
    .join("\n");
}

+# Architect answers - S4-05b phase 1 (2026-10-16)
+
+Authority: `.agent/architect-S4-05b-request.md`, the S4-05 shared contract and S4-05b packet, `D-2026-10-15-s405-stored-demo-provenance`, the phase-1 handoff, and the owner's T028 presentation direction. These are interim implementation rulings. The owner still approves final visible wording at the W1 copy checkpoint before S4-05b merges.
+
+## 1. Source wording and placement
+
+Use these interim operator-facing labels on Finding, Evidence, consequence, Financials, and S5 Overview wherever the selected source state is shown:
+
+| Stored state | Default label | Adjacent plain statement |
+| --- | --- | --- |
+| `AUTHORED_DEMONSTRATION` | `Prepared example data` | `These records were prepared for an example. They are not readings from physical equipment.` |
+| `REGISTERED_SIMULATION` | `Generated test data` | `These records were generated for a test. They are not readings from physical equipment.` |
+| `MIXED` | `Sources do not agree` | `The records behind this result have different or unverified sources. No current cost is shown.` |
+| `UNKNOWN` | `Source unverified` | `We cannot establish the source of all records behind this result. No current cost is shown.` |
+
+Show the label and statement together near the Finding or Evidence result and next to any eligible current amount or withheld-current-cost state. The label must remain visible without opening a disclosure, including at narrow width and to assistive technology. A positive authored or generated designation applies only to the exact selected citation set (and the consequence's additional power records), not the Site or all its records. For `MIXED` and `UNKNOWN`, do not imply the records are physical, generated, or zero cost. Historical amounts remain labelled historical and audit-only with the exact revision and its own source state.
+
+Put the detailed per-record source, publication, fixture/version or recorded-run identity, reasons and exact IDs in the existing deeper source detail disclosure or Analysis layer. Explain the method there in ordinary language; raw API enum values and IDs may remain in machine-readable data, but do not print the enum or implementation words as product prose. The T028 vocabulary guard scans disclosures and accessible labels as well as default text. The four labels and statements above avoid its banned terms; no vocabulary exemption is authorized. In particular, `Simulated data` must not be rendered, and `Demonstration data` is replaced by the more explicit prepared-example wording. The source-owned identifier, user-owned and Lab exemptions remain limited to their existing purposes, not source-state copy. Test populated positive, mixed and unknown states with the product vocabulary scan. The final wording, placement and visual treatment remain the owner's W1-conclusion copy checkpoint.
+
+## 2. Partly sealed registered Lab stage
+
+Accept the phase-1 fail-closed change. A registered Lab stage may be committed only after every batch in that stage is sealed and can receive the immutable pre-admission publication. If any batch is still sealing or was refused, return `SOURCE_PUBLICATION_REFUSED` and release none of the stage. Do not publish or upload the sealed subset as an implicit partial Commit. A later explicit retry may succeed only after the whole stage meets the same rule; do not backfill or relabel already admitted evidence. Keep the refused state visible in Lab so the operator knows that no partial release occurred. This is a deliberate change from the former "as far as sealed" behavior and needs the phase-2 walkthrough/regression proof.
+
+## 3. Action source cutover
+
+Yes. Phase 2 must remove `demonstration_label.py` runtime use from `actions/service.py` as part of the same heuristic retirement. The recommendation and persisted Action detail must obtain source state for their exact pinned origin FindingRevision from the stored provenance resolver, preserving that revision and its evidence IDs. Do not decide an Action's source from Site, episode dates, a fixture file or the latest Finding head. If the resolver is unwired, fails, or cannot resolve the pinned revision, show `Source unverified` and the typed source limitation; do not return an authored/generated label. Keep any Action application, capability and outcome rules governed by their existing contracts; this ruling changes source presentation and its read basis only. Update Action API/UI consumers and focused regressions so no product read still uses the date/spec heuristic. The owner reviews Action-visible copy with the same W1 wording checkpoint.

 Decision: `D-2026-10-16-w1-remaining-questions`. Accept the five remaining W1 packet recommendations with bounded clarifications: S6-03 cursors fence all Actions and their events in the selected Site/creation window, including off-page rows, with maximum creation/event-recorded times and Action/event counts, and invalidate a later page when the fence changes; S7-01a uses a versioned two-clock health DTO and one repeatable-read registration/diagnostic snapshot without changing S2 gateway-health in place; S7-01b keys battery detail to the effective configured asset, shows `NOT_CONFIGURED` when absent, and refuses foreign or superseded asset IDs; S3-02a returns exact role-coverage spans and durations under one versioned rule without masking missing/conflicted subspans; S3-02b keeps S2 explanation separate and shows an explicit `UNASSESSED` S3 section until an assessment revision is selected. Implementation detail and review gates remain in the packets. Full answers: `.agent/architect-W1-remaining-q-answers.md`.
+
+Decision: `D-2026-10-16-s405b-label`. For S4-05b, use interim operator labels `Prepared example data` for exact authored evidence, `Generated test data` for exact registered generated evidence, `Sources do not agree` for mixed evidence, and `Source unverified` for unknown evidence, each with adjacent plain source-limit text. Positive labels explicitly say the records are not readings from physical equipment; mixed/unknown never imply a physical source or zero cost. Keep exact source IDs and basis in deeper detail, with no vocabulary-scan exemption for source-state prose. A partly sealed registered Lab stage refuses the entire Commit with `SOURCE_PUBLICATION_REFUSED`; no sealed subset is released. Phase 2 also retires `demonstration_label.py` from Action reads and resolves each Action's pinned origin FindingRevision, failing closed to unknown when unresolved. Full ruling: `.agent/architect-S4-05b-answers.md`. Final visible wording and placement remain the owner's W1 copy checkpoint before S4-05b merge.

# Architect answers - S4-05b phase 1 (2026-10-16)

Authority: `.agent/architect-S4-05b-request.md`, the S4-05 shared contract and S4-05b packet, `D-2026-10-15-s405-stored-demo-provenance`, the phase-1 handoff, and the owner's T028 presentation direction. These are interim implementation rulings. The owner still approves final visible wording at the W1 copy checkpoint before S4-05b merges.

## 1. Source wording and placement

Use these interim operator-facing labels on Finding, Evidence, consequence, Financials, and S5 Overview wherever the selected source state is shown:


diff --git a/.agent/architect-S4-05b-answers.md b/.agent/architect-S4-05b-answers.md

+# Architect answers - S4-05b phase 1 (2026-10-16)
+
+Authority: `.agent/architect-S4-05b-request.md`, the S4-05 shared contract and S4-05b packet, `D-2026-10-15-s405-stored-demo-provenance`, the phase-1 handoff, and the owner's T028 presentation direction. These are interim implementation rulings. The owner still approves final visible wording at the W1 copy checkpoint before S4-05b merges.
+
+## 1. Source wording and placement
+
+Use these interim operator-facing labels on Finding, Evidence, consequence, Financials, and S5 Overview wherever the selected source state is shown:
+
+| Stored state | Default label | Adjacent plain statement |
+| --- | --- | --- |
+| `AUTHORED_DEMONSTRATION` | `Prepared example data` | `These records were prepared for an example. They are not readings from physical equipment.` |
+| `REGISTERED_SIMULATION` | `Generated test data` | `These records were generated for a test. They are not readings from physical equipment.` |
+| `MIXED` | `Sources do not agree` | `The records behind this result have different or unverified sources. No current cost is shown.` |
+| `UNKNOWN` | `Source unverified` | `We cannot establish the source of all records behind this result. No current cost is shown.` |
+
+Show the label and statement together near the Finding or Evidence result and next to any eligible current amount or withheld-current-cost state. The label must remain visible without opening a disclosure, including at narrow width and to assistive technology. A positive authored or generated designation applies only to the exact selected citation set (and the consequence's additional power records), not the Site or all its records. For `MIXED` and `UNKNOWN`, do not imply the records are physical, generated, or zero cost. Historical amounts remain labelled historical and audit-only with the exact revision and its own source state.
+
+Put the detailed per-record source, publication, fixture/version or recorded-run identity, reasons and exact IDs in the existing deeper source detail disclosure or Analysis layer. Explain the method there in ordinary language; raw API enum values and IDs may remain in machine-readable data, but do not print the enum or implementation words as product prose. The T028 vocabulary guard scans disclosures and accessible labels as well as default text. The four labels and statements above avoid its banned terms; no vocabulary exemption is authorized. In particular, `Simulated data` must not be rendered, and `Demonstration data` is replaced by the more explicit prepared-example wording. The source-owned identifier, user-owned and Lab exemptions remain limited to their existing purposes, not source-state copy. Test populated positive, mixed and unknown states with the product vocabulary scan. The final wording, placement and visual treatment remain the owner's W1-conclusion copy checkpoint.
+
+## 2. Partly sealed registered Lab stage
+
+Accept the phase-1 fail-closed change. A registered Lab stage may be committed only after every batch in that stage is sealed and can receive the immutable pre-admission publication. If any batch is still sealing or was refused, return `SOURCE_PUBLICATION_REFUSED` and release none of the stage. Do not publish or upload the sealed subset as an implicit partial Commit. A later explicit retry may succeed only after the whole stage meets the same rule; do not backfill or relabel already admitted evidence. Keep the refused state visible in Lab so the operator knows that no partial release occurred. This is a deliberate change from the former "as far as sealed" behavior and needs the phase-2 walkthrough/regression proof.
+
+## 3. Action source cutover
+
+Yes. Phase 2 must remove `demonstration_label.py` runtime use from `actions/service.py` as part of the same heuristic retirement. The recommendation and persisted Action detail must obtain source state for their exact pinned origin FindingRevision from the stored provenance resolver, preserving that revision and its evidence IDs. Do not decide an Action's source from Site, episode dates, a fixture file or the latest Finding head. If the resolver is unwired, fails, or cannot resolve the pinned revision, show `Source unverified` and the typed source limitation; do not return an authored/generated label. Keep any Action application, capability and outcome rules governed by their existing contracts; this ruling changes source presentation and its read basis only. Update Action API/UI consumers and focused regressions so no product read still uses the date/spec heuristic. The owner reviews Action-visible copy with the same W1 wording checkpoint.

For S4-05b, use interim operator labels `Prepared example data` for exact authored evidence, `Generated test data` for exact registered generated evidence, `Sources do not agree` for mixed evidence, and `Source unverified` for unknown evidence, each with adjacent plain source-limit text. Positive labels explicitly say the records are not readings from physical equipment; mixed/unknown never imply a physical source or zero cost. Keep exact source IDs and basis in deeper detail, with no vocabulary-scan exemption for source-state prose. A partly sealed registered Lab stage refuses the entire Commit with `SOURCE_PUBLICATION_REFUSED`; no sealed subset is released. Phase 2 also retires `demonstration_label.py` from Action reads and resolves each Action's pinned origin FindingRevision, failing closed to unknown when unresolved. Full ruling: `.agent/architect-S4-05b-answers.md`. Final visible wording and placement remain the owner's W1 copy checkpoint before S4-05b merge.

+# Architect answers - S4-05b phase 1 (2026-10-16)
+
+Authority: `.agent/architect-S4-05b-request.md`, the S4-05 shared contract and S4-05b packet, `D-2026-10-15-s405-stored-demo-provenance`, the phase-1 handoff, and the owner's T028 presentation direction. These are interim implementation rulings. The owner still approves final visible wording at the W1 copy checkpoint before S4-05b merges.
+
+## 1. Source wording and placement
+
+Use these interim operator-facing labels on Finding, Evidence, consequence, Financials, and S5 Overview wherever the selected source state is shown:
+
+| Stored state | Default label | Adjacent plain statement |
+| --- | --- | --- |
+| `AUTHORED_DEMONSTRATION` | `Prepared example data` | `These records were prepared for an example. They are not readings from physical equipment.` |
+| `REGISTERED_SIMULATION` | `Generated test data` | `These records were generated for a test. They are not readings from physical equipment.` |
+| `MIXED` | `Sources do not agree` | `The records behind this result have different or unverified sources. No current cost is shown.` |
+| `UNKNOWN` | `Source unverified` | `We cannot establish the source of all records behind this result. No current cost is shown.` |
+
+Show the label and statement together near the Finding or Evidence result and next to any eligible current amount or withheld-current-cost state. The label must remain visible without opening a disclosure, including at narrow width and to assistive technology. A positive authored or generated designation applies only to the exact selected citation set (and the consequence's additional power records), not the Site or all its records. For `MIXED` and `UNKNOWN`, do not imply the records are physical, generated, or zero cost. Historical amounts remain labelled historical and audit-only with the exact revision and its own source state.
+
+Put the detailed per-record source, publication, fixture/version or recorded-run identity, reasons and exact IDs in the existing deeper source detail disclosure or Analysis layer. Explain the method there in ordinary language; raw API enum values and IDs may remain in machine-readable data, but do not print the enum or implementation words as product prose. The T028 vocabulary guard scans disclosures and accessible labels as well as default text. The four labels and statements above avoid its banned terms; no vocabulary exemption is authorized. In particular, `Simulated data` must not be rendered, and `Demonstration data` is replaced by the more explicit prepared-example wording. The source-owned identifier, user-owned and Lab exemptions remain limited to their existing purposes, not source-state copy. Test populated positive, mixed and unknown states with the product vocabulary scan. The final wording, placement and visual treatment remain the owner's W1-conclusion copy checkpoint.
+
+## 2. Partly sealed registered Lab stage
+
+Accept the phase-1 fail-closed change. A registered Lab stage may be committed only after every batch in that stage is sealed and can receive the immutable pre-admission publication. If any batch is still sealing or was refused, return `SOURCE_PUBLICATION_REFUSED` and release none of the stage. Do not publish or upload the sealed subset as an implicit partial Commit. A later explicit retry may succeed only after the whole stage meets the same rule; do not backfill or relabel already admitted evidence. Keep the refused state visible in Lab so the operator knows that no partial release occurred. This is a deliberate change from the former "as far as sealed" behavior and needs the phase-2 walkthrough/regression proof.
+
+## 3. Action source cutover
+
+Yes. Phase 2 must remove `demonstration_label.py` runtime use from `actions/service.py` as part of the same heuristic retirement. The recommendation and persisted Action detail must obtain source state for their exact pinned origin FindingRevision from the stored provenance resolver, preserving that revision and its evidence IDs. Do not decide an Action's source from Site, episode dates, a fixture file or the latest Finding head. If the resolver is unwired, fails, or cannot resolve the pinned revision, show `Source unverified` and the typed source limitation; do not return an authored/generated label. Keep any Action application, capability and outcome rules governed by their existing contracts; this ruling changes source presentation and its read basis only. Update Action API/UI consumers and focused regressions so no product read still uses the date/spec heuristic. The owner reviews Action-visible copy with the same W1 wording checkpoint.
